## 0. Legend

### Status
| Mark | Status | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `[ ]` | TODO | Not started, unclaimed |
| `[~]` | IN-PROGRESS | Claimed and being worked on — **must** have an owner |
| `[?]` | REVIEW | Work finished, awaiting verification/review by someone else |
| `[!]` | BLOCKED | Cannot proceed — **must** state the blocker |
| `[x]` | DONE | Verified complete (tests pass / acceptance check met) |
| `[-]` | DROPPED | Decided not to do — **must** state why |

### Priority (work in this order)
| Priority | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **P0** | Critical — broken, security risk, or blocks other work. Do first. |
| **P1** | High — required for the milestone to be considered done. |
| **P2** | Medium — should do this milestone; can slip one. |
| **P3** | Low — nice-to-have / backlog. |

### IDs
`M<milestone>.<task>.<subtask>` — e.g. `M1.6.2`. `X` = cross-cutting. IDs are **permanent**: never renumber or reuse; append new items with the next free number. Legacy IDs from the first version of this plan are shown as `(was 1.7)`.

### Task line format
```
- [~] **M1.6** Task title — `P1` — owner: @name — since: 2026-10-01 — deps: M0.4 — spec: §5.2.2
  - [?] **M1.6.1** Subtask title — `P1`
```
`spec:` is the spec section the task satisfies; `code:` (when present) is the existing place to extend.

## 1. Rules for multiple agents / developers

### 1.1 Before starting work
1. **Pull / re-read `plan.md` fresh.** Never act on a cached copy — another agent may have changed it.
2. **Pick the highest-priority unclaimed task** whose `deps` are all `[x]`. Order: P0 → P1 → P2 → P3, then lowest milestone, then lowest ID.
3. **Claim it first, then work.** Set `[~]`, `owner: @you`, `since: <date>` and save `plan.md` *before* touching any code. A claim is the lock.
4. **One task in progress per agent/developer.** Finish, hand off, or release before claiming another.
5. **Never take over a claimed task.** A claim older than **48 h** with no Work Log entry is stale: add a Work Log note ("releasing stale claim on X") and then you may re-claim it.

### 1.2 While working
6. **Autosave the plan:** update `plan.md` immediately after *every* status change — claiming, finishing a subtask, getting blocked, or finding new work. Do not batch updates to the end.
7. **Stay inside your task's file scope.** If you must edit files another `[~]` task owns, coordinate first (Work Log) or mark yourself `[!]` BLOCKED on that task.
8. **Discovered work goes into the plan, not into your diff.** Add a new `[ ]` task/subtask with a priority and continue. Do not silently expand scope.
9. **Blocked?** Set `[!]`, write `blocked: <reason / who or what you're waiting on>`, release ownership if you won't come back, and move to the next task.

### 1.3 Finishing
10. **Done means verified.** `[x]` only when the acceptance check passes (tests green, smoke test run, or reviewer sign-off). Otherwise use `[?]`.
11. **No self-review for P0/P1 code changes.** A different agent/developer moves `[?]` → `[x]`.
12. **The test baseline must not regress.** Run `pytest -q` from the repo root before marking anything done; update the "Test baseline" field if the count changes.
13. **Append to the Work Log** (§5) — one line per session: date, who, task IDs, outcome.

### 1.4 Editing this file
14. **Edit only your own lines** (your claimed task, your Work Log entry, new tasks you add). Don't reorder, reword, or re-prioritize others' tasks — propose changes in the Decisions log (§3).
15. **Priority/milestone changes and dropping tasks** need a Decisions-log entry with the reason.
16. **Merge conflicts in `plan.md`:** keep *both* sides' Work Log entries; for a task's status take the most advanced state only if its owner agrees; never delete someone else's claim.
17. **Never record secrets** (tokens, passwords, API keys, real `.env` values) in this file.

### 1.5 Repo safety (all contributors)
18. **Default-off:** new capabilities (retrieval, uploads, tools) ship disabled and must not change existing endpoint/test behavior when off.
19. **Schema changes** go through a new Alembic migration (`YYYYMMDD_NNNN_slug.py`) — never edit an applied migration.
20. **Don't commit** `*.db`, `*.db.bak`, `.venv*/`, `__pycache__/`, or `.env`.

### 1.6 Research-integrity rules (from the spec's principles)
21. **Gates can't be bypassed.** No agent or code path may approve a gate on a human's behalf, auto-approve on timeout, or proceed past a `blocked` state (spec §8).
22. **Fail loudly.** On LLM or retrieval failure, record the failure and stop the step. Never write canned/placeholder content and mark the step done (spec principle 2).
23. **Retrieved text is untrusted data.** Web/PDF/abstract text is fenced and labelled in prompts and never treated as instructions (see M1.2).
24. **Numbers come from code; citations come from verified records.** No generated statistic or reference is stored as verified without a computed source or a resolved record (spec §7.3–7.4).
25. **Every AI output records** `model_id`, `prompt_version`, inputs and actor (once M0.4/M0.8 land; new AI features must not ship without them).
26. **Don't import ARC pipeline code** (it fails open and auto-approves gates). Port ideas; call ARC only through its HTTP retrieval service.

### 1.7 Oversight rules (product-owner sign-off)
27. **Definition of done.** A milestone is done only when its spec §12 exit criterion and the related §13 acceptance criteria pass. Each `M*.exit` task names the criterion it proves.
28. **Milestone report.** Every milestone (and every demo) ends with a report in `milestone-reports/` using [TEMPLATE.md](milestone-reports/TEMPLATE.md): features vs [traceability.md](docs/traceability.md), test + gold-set results, known issues/deferrals, spec deviations.
29. **Non-negotiables at every demo.** Record pass / fail / not-built for N1–N5 (`docs/traceability.md` §3): citations click through and unverifiable ones are blocked; AI outputs show producer/model/date/approval; gates block; earlier-stage changes mark downstream stale; draft numbers link to analysis runs.
30. **Short cycles.** A working demo every 1–2 weeks, in milestone order (M0 → M3 first). No big reveal at the end.
31. **Traceability stays current.** Update `docs/traceability.md` whenever a task is added, finished or dropped. Spec lines with no task are gaps (add a task); tasks with no spec line go in its "no spec line" section with a reason. A deliberate spec change goes in the Decisions log **and** is edited into the spec.
32. **UI starts from a wireframe.** No screen in M2+ is built before its wireframe has been walked through by the scholar (X.14).

### 1.8 Local working rules (developers & agents on one machine)

**Claiming (works for any tool or person, not just Claude)**
- Every agent/IDE/human follows the same claim steps, whichever instruction file their tool reads (`CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, Cursor/Copilot rules, or none). `AGENTS.md` is the canonical copy; if you can't see the rules, open `plan.md` §1 yourself.
- To claim, hand-edit your task line in `plan.md`: `[~]`, `owner: @name`, `since: YYYY-MM-DD`; save the file; then start work. No tooling needed.
- **Claims are only visible inside one working tree.** Developers on separate clones/machines can't see each other's claims until `plan.md` is shared (a commit/push the user approves). Before claiming, ask the user whether others are working elsewhere; if so, agree a task split in chat first and write it in the Work Log.

**Separate clones (Codex, another IDE, another person)**
- A claim only counts once the other clone can see it. With separate clones the claim must travel through git: a **claim-only commit** touching nothing but your `plan.md` task line, pushed to the shared base branch (`main`) *before* you code. This is a git action: do it only when the user approves it, and `git pull` (or `git fetch` + read `origin/main:plan.md`) before choosing so you see others' claims.
- If the user doesn't want claim commits, use a manual split instead: the user assigns task IDs per person/tool in chat, and each clone marks only its assigned tasks. Never pick a task outside your assignment.
- Prefer disjoint areas: assign by milestone or file scope so two clones never edit the same files. Overlapping files across clones is resolved by the user, not by whoever pushes first.
- Merge `plan.md` per rule 16 (keep both Work Log lines; never drop another clone's claim).

**Multiple developers on different branches**
- Each developer's `plan.md` lives on their own branch, so a claim on your branch is invisible to everyone else until it reaches `main`. **The source of truth for claims is `origin/main:plan.md`**, not your branch copy.
- One branch per task, named `<owner>/<task-id>-<slug>` (e.g. `revati/M2.3-screening-ui`), created from current `main`. Don't stack unrelated tasks on one branch.
- Before choosing: `git fetch`, then read `origin/main:plan.md` (`git show origin/main:plan.md`) and every open branch/PR title for active claims. Skip any task claimed there or whose files overlap an open branch.
- To claim: a **claim-only change** (your task line in `plan.md`, nothing else) must land on `main` first, via a tiny PR or direct push. Then branch from that `main`. Git actions need the user's approval each time (rule 41). If claims can't be pushed, the user assigns task IDs in chat instead and you work only on those.
- Keep `plan.md` edits on a task branch limited to your own lines (status, subtasks, Work Log) to keep merge conflicts trivial; resolve them per rule 16.
- Before opening a PR: fetch, check `main` didn't claim or finish the same task meanwhile, and confirm your files don't collide with another open branch. Finished status (`[?]`) goes in with the PR; `[x]` is set by a reviewer after merge.
- Branch protection: don't merge your own P0/P1 PR (rule 11); don't push directly to `main` except claim-only changes the user approved.

**Integrating into `main` (rules 47–56; git actions still need user approval, rule 41)**
*Rules 47–51 apply only when a branch/PR workflow is used. When developers work directly on `main`, follow §1.10 (rules 66–81) instead; rules 52–56 apply in both workflows.*
47. **`main` is the only integration point.** No long-lived shared dev branch. Task branches are short-lived (aim ≤ 3 days) and cut from the latest `main`.
48. **Land via PR only.** One task branch → one PR to `main`. Title starts with the task ID; the description lists files touched, tests run, the `pytest -q` result and the baseline delta.
49. **Sync, then test.** Before the PR: fetch and merge `main` into your branch (**merge only — never rebase**, and never rewrite history on a pushed branch), then re-run `pytest -q` *after* the sync, not before.
50. **Merge gate.** Merge only if: full `pytest -q` green, baseline not regressed, a different person/agent approved (rule 11), `plan.md` conflicts resolved per rule 16, and `alembic heads` shows exactly one head.
51. **Merge order.** P0 first, then lowest milestone/ID; a task waits for its deps' PRs. When two PRs conflict, the first merged wins and the second syncs onto it. Nobody force-pushes `main`.
52. **Migrations are serialized.** Two branches must not both ship the same `NNNN`. The second to merge renumbers its migration, re-verifies up/down/up on a scratch DB, and re-checks the single head.
53. **`plan.md` after merge.** The merger updates the "Test baseline" field in the merge; the reviewer sets `[x]`. Every branch's Work Log lines are kept.
54. **Hotspot files** (`models.py`, `schemas.py`, `main.py`, `config.py`, `plan.md`, `docs/traceability.md`): make small, additive edits. The later PR resolves the conflict; it never drops the earlier side's change.
55. **After merge.** Delete the task branch (user-approved); others fetch `main` and re-check claims. A broken `main` is P0: revert first, fix second.
56. **Unmerged work is visible.** `git branch -r --no-merged origin/main` lists it. Branches idle > 7 days with no Work Log entry are reported to the user, never deleted by an agent.

**Choosing a task**
33. **Check before you choose.** Run `git status` and `git log -5 --oneline`, then re-read `plan.md`. List every `[~]` task and the files it owns; do not pick a task whose files overlap an active claim.
34. **No claim, no code.** If you can't claim a task (nothing eligible, all deps open), stop and report — never start "small" unplanned edits. Docs/rules-only edits still get a task line.
35. **Ambiguous or gated?** Tasks that need a scholar decision (Q-items in §4, gates, wireframes) are `[!]`, not guessable. Ask the user; don't assume the answer.

**Executing**
36. **Smallest change that meets the acceptance check.** Touch only files in your task's scope; tests mirror `app/<area>.py` as `test_<area>.py`.
37. **Verify in layers.** Run the task's focused tests while working; run full `pytest -q` from the repo root before `[?]`/`[x]`. Report failures with their output — never mark done on a red or unrun suite, and say plainly when the full suite was not run.
38. **Use `.venv` only** (never `.venv-retrieval`). Never run destructive commands against a real `.env` or non-scratch database; migrations are verified on a scratch SQLite DB.
39. **Stop and hand off cleanly.** At session end the plan, Work Log and your task status must match reality (`[~]` only if you'll return; otherwise `[?]`, `[!]` or release to `[ ]`).

**Git status & the working tree**
40. **The tree is shared and often dirty.** Uncommitted changes you didn't make belong to someone else's task. Never revert, stash, reset, checkout or delete them. Edit around them; if your task needs the same file, coordinate via the Work Log first (rule 7).
41. **Read-only git by default.** `status`, `diff`, `log`, `show`, `blame` are always fine. **Commit, push, branch, merge, rebase, stash, tag, reset and `--force` anything only when the user explicitly asks, per action.** Approval doesn't carry over to the next action.
42. **Commits are always manual, made by the developer.** Agents leave changes in the working tree and hand off a list of changed files plus a suggested commit message; they do not commit unless that developer explicitly asks in that session. **If you do commit:** stage explicit paths only (never `git add -A` / `.`), review `git diff --staged`, and include only files from your task. One task → one commit; message starts with the task ID (e.g. `M1.2.5: …`). Never `--no-verify` or amend others' commits.
43. **Never stage or commit** `*.db`, `*.db.bak`, `.venv*/`, `__pycache__/`, `.env`, secrets, or `.claude/` local settings (rule 20).
44. **Untracked ≠ junk.** New untracked files (new routers, migrations, tests) may be another task's unfinished work; don't delete or "tidy" them. Before creating a migration, list `backend/migrations/versions/` for the next free `NNNN`.
45. **Report the tree honestly.** In your handoff note which files *you* changed, run `git status --short` last, and call out anything unexpected rather than cleaning it up.
46. **Base-branch sync is the user's call.** Don't pull/merge/rebase `main` into your branch unprompted; if a conflict touches `plan.md`, follow rule 16.
57. **Step-by-step order (owner, 2026-10-10).** No new stage's screens while the current stage has open red items; verified extraction and evidence (M3.3, M3.4, M3.11, M3.14, M3.6) come before more screens or analysis; the screening screen (M2.3) is never built before the X.14.2 walkthrough; never weaken the citation block, gates, append-only audit, untrusted-text fencing, no-placeholder-on-failure, or numbers-only-from-code. Every claim of "works" says whether it was tested offline only, on SQLite only, or on a live API/model.

### 1.9 Reviewing other agents' work (owner request, 2026-10-14)

Anything built by one agent or person is reviewed by a different one. These rules say how to pick that review and what to record.

58. **Review before you build.** After re-reading `plan.md` (rule 1), list every `[?]` task. If any was built by someone else, claim one review before claiming new build work. Order: P0 → P1 → P2 → P3, then lowest milestone, then lowest ID. P0/P1 reviews are required by rule 11, so they come first. The only exception is a task the user assigned you by ID in chat.
59. **Never review your own work.** A task is not eligible if its `owner:` is you, if the Work Log shows you built any part of it, or if you wrote code for it in this session. A task owned by `@claude` can be reviewed by `@codex`, `@copilot` or a person, and the reverse. If every `[?]` task is yours, say so and ask the user for a named reviewer; do not review your own.
60. **Claim the review.** Leave the status at `[?]`. Add `reviewer: @you — review since: YYYY-MM-DD` to the task line and save `plan.md` before reading any code. One review at a time. A review claim older than 48 h with no Work Log entry is stale (rule 5).
61. **Re-verify; don't trust the author's evidence.** Read the task line's claim, the code and its tests. Run the focused tests, then the full `pytest -q` from the repo root. Check the change against rules 21–26. Where the task says a test guards a behaviour, repeat the mutation check (the test must fail without the change). Say whether each result is offline only, SQLite only, or on a live API/model/Postgres.
62. **Findings, not fixes.** Do not edit the author's code during a review. Record a defect as a finding on the task line, or as a new task (rule 8), and leave the status at `[?]`. Fix it in a later session only if the user asks.
63. **Outcomes.** Accept → `[x]`, but only on evidence you obtained in this environment. If part of the check cannot run here (a GitHub Actions run, a browser check, Postgres, a live model), leave `[?]` and state exactly what is missing. Tasks that need a named human (X.14.2, X.15, gate approvals, Q-items) cannot be accepted by an agent.
64. **Record the review.** On the task line: `reviewed YYYY-MM-DD by @you: result; tests run (counts); caveats`, and remove your `reviewer:` field. In the Work Log: one line with the task IDs, the outcome and the test counts. In the header table: update `Last updated`, `Updated by`, the awaiting-review part of `Current focus`, and `Test baseline` if the count changed.
65. **Nothing to review?** Say so in your handoff and continue with rule 2.

### 1.10 Two developers on `main` (different machines)

Use this when two people (or their agents) commit straight to `main` from separate clones. It replaces rules 47–51 for that setup; rules 11, 16, 41, 52–56 still apply. Git actions still need the user's approval (rule 41).

**Avoiding overlapping work**
66. **The claim lands on `origin/main` before you code.** Make a claim-only commit touching just your `plan.md` task line, `git pull --rebase`, then push. If the push is rejected, someone claimed first: re-pull and pick another task. That push is the lock (extends rule 3 and §1.8).
67. **Split by area.** Agree an area per person (milestone or directory, e.g. backend routers and migrations vs frontend and docs) and record it in the Work Log. A task outside your area needs a chat message to the other developer first.
68. **Hotspot files get a mini-claim.** Before editing `models.py`, `schemas.py`, `main.py`, `config.py`, `plan.md` or `docs/traceability.md`, add a one-line Work Log note ("editing models.py, ~1h"). Keep edits small and additive (rule 54).
69. **Migration numbers are reserved in the claim.** Put the next free `NNNN` in your claim commit, and run `alembic heads` after every pull; it must show one head (rules 52, X.64). A new revision id is appended to `backend/migrations/revision_ids.txt`.
70. **Chat handshake.** Say "starting <task>" and "pushed <task>" in your team chat. This backs up `plan.md`; it does not replace it.

**Keeping git runs from failing**
71. **Sync cadence.** `git pull --rebase --autostash` before starting, before every commit and before every push. Rebasing is allowed here only for your own unpushed commits (this narrows rule 49 for direct-to-`main` work).
72. **Small, frequent commits.** One task per commit, message starts with the task ID. Push at the end of every session; unpushed work older than a day counts as overlap risk. Only push when your local `pytest -q` is green (rule 12).
73. **Never rewrite shared history.** No force-push to `main`, no `reset --hard` or `checkout --` on files you didn't change, no amending a pushed commit. If a push is rejected: pull, rebase, push again. If that fails twice, stop and tell the other developer.
74. **Conflict protocol.** Whoever hits the conflict resolves it. In `plan.md` keep both Work Log lines and never drop the other person's claim (rule 16). In code keep both sides' intent and re-run `pytest -q` before continuing. If you can't tell what the other side meant, ask; don't guess.
75. **Line endings and paths.** Both machines set `core.autocrlf=input`, and the repo carries a `.gitattributes` with `* text=auto eol=lf` so Windows and other systems stop producing whole-file diffs. Use forward-slash paths, don't rename files by case only, and use the same Python version and `.venv` setup.
76. **Runtime data stays out of git.** `backend/data/objects/projects/*/`, `*.db`, `*.db.bak`, `.venv*/`, `.env` and `.claude/` are never committed (rules 20, 43). They belong in `.gitignore`; until that exists, never stage them.
77. **Clean tree before pulling.** Commit your own finished work or let `--autostash` hold it. Never stash, revert or delete changes you didn't make (rules 40, 44).
78. **A red `main` is P0.** If a pull leaves `pytest -q` red, tell the other developer at once. The person who pushed last reverts first and fixes second (rule 55). Nobody starts new work until `main` is green.

**Review and handoff**
79. **Cross-review.** Each developer reviews the other's `[?]` tasks (§1.9), after a pull so the review is of what is actually on `main`.
80. **End of session.** Pull, run the tests, push, then update `plan.md` and the Work Log (rule 39). Don't leave `[~]` on a task you won't return to within 48 h (rule 5).
81. **Switching workflow.** If the team moves to task branches and PRs, rules 47–51 apply again from that date; record the change in the Decisions log (§3).

## 2. Milestones, tasks & subtasks (in priority order)

Work order (restructured 2026-10-15, owner request): **M0 → Stage 1 literature gathering (M1 + M2) → M4 methodology → M3 optional evidence synthesis → M5–M7**. The researcher gives the brief (topic, keywords, criteria, scope, citation threshold); AI searches and ranks; the researcher retains/rejects and can add own papers; G3 locks the literature; methodology follows. M0–M3 are fully broken down. M4–M7 and X are task-level only; break them into subtasks when a milestone is about to start (add them under the existing task, next free ID).

### M0 — Foundations · status: `[x]`
Spec: §3, §4, §8, §10, Appendix C · exit: *create a project, share it, log events, run a gated task* (spec M0). **Closed 2026-10-09: M0.1–M0.11 all `[x]`.** Milestone report refresh (rule 28, X.13) and traceability update (rule 31, X.12) deliberately left as their own open tasks, not done here.

- [x] **M0.1** Version control & workspace hygiene — `P0` — done; detail: `docs/plan-archive/tasks-done.md`
  - [x] **M0.1.1** `git init` (or clone the upstream remote — see Q1) (was 0.1.1) — `P0` — done; detail: `docs/plan-archive/tasks-done.md`

Verified: offline Postgres SQL for 0008→0010 has each `CREATE TYPE` once; scratch SQLite with a pre-existing project → 11 gates, down/up clean, `alembic check` clean; `pytest -q` → 227 passed. Live Postgres still not run (X.1). SQLite behaviour unchanged, so already-upgraded dev DBs are unaffected.) Fix migration `20261003_0009` on Postgres: it calls `gate_code`/`gate_status` `.create(checkfirst=True)` and then `op.create_table`, which emits `CREATE TYPE` again → "type already exists", upgrade aborts. Offline `alembic upgrade 20261003_0008:20261003_0009 --sql` against a Postgres URL shows each `CREATE TYPE` twice. Fix: pass `postgresql.ENUM(..., create_type=False)` (or drop the explicit `.create()`); check M0.6.1's migration for the same pattern. Minor: the backfill's `bind.execute(...).fetchall()` breaks offline `--sql` generation. Found in review of M0.5.2 (review otherwise clean: model, policy, unique constraint, bootstrap and `ensure_gates` match the task; 5/5 tests pass; full suite 223 passed).   - [x] **M0.5.9** (owner: @claude-reviewer — since: 2026-10-03 — needs review (P1): `decide_gate` now writes with `UPDATE gates … WHERE id = :id AND status != 'approved'` and returns 409 (rolling back, no audit event) when 0 rows change, so the database re-checks at write time; `ensure_gates` inserts missing gates inside a savepoint and re-reads on `IntegrityError`. Two new tests in `test_gate_decisions.py` on a file-backed SQLite fixture (`race_db`, separate connections — the shared in-memory StaticPool engine can't interleave sessions): concurrent approve-vs-reject → second gets 409, gate stays approved, one audit event; stale first read in `ensure_gates` → no error, 11 gates. **Mutation-checked:** removing the WHERE guard fails the race test; not catching `IntegrityError` fails the creation test. The human-only guard test's pattern was widened to also catch keyword/bulk writes (`status=outcome`, `update(Gate)`) and was checked by planting `update(Gate)` in `worker.py`. Postgres not run (X.1); READ COMMITTED re-evaluates the WHERE after a row lock, so the same guard holds there.) Gate decisions race: `decide_gate` reads the gate, checks "not approved", then writes, with no lock or conditional update. Reproduced on scratch SQLite with two sessions: owner approves G2 and supervisor rejects it concurrently → both succeed, final status `rejected`, audit shows `gate.approved` then `gate.rejected`. The 409 "already approved" rule is bypassed under concurrency. Fix: conditional `UPDATE gates SET … WHERE id = :id AND status != 'approved'` and 409 when 0 rows (portable), or `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on Postgres; add a two-session test. Also: concurrent first `GET /gates` on a project with no gate rows can hit `uq_project_gate` → 500 (handle IntegrityError in `ensure_gates`). Found in review of M0.5.3 — `P1` — done; detail: `docs/plan-archive/tasks-done.md`

  - [x] **M0.6.1** `Task(id, project_id, type, payload, status, attempts, max_attempts, lease_expires_at, blocked_by_gate, error)` + migration — `P1` — done; detail: `docs/plan-archive/tasks-done.md`
  - [x] **M0.6.8** (owner: @claude-reviewer — since: 2026-10-03 — needs review (P1): every state change in `app/task_queue.py` now goes through `_transition()`, a conditional `UPDATE … WHERE id = :id AND <precondition>` that checks the row count: claim (`status='queued' AND attempts=:seen`, retried up to 5× if another worker won), heartbeat/complete/fail/claim-backstop (`status='running' AND attempts=:attempt`), reaper (also `lease_expires_at < now`, so a heartbeat that lands first wins), block/pause (`status='queued'`), resume (`status='paused'`). A refused write changes nothing and writes no audit event; the reaper returns how many it actually reaped. `_owned_running_task` removed (no other users). Public API unchanged. 4 interleaving tests in `test_task_queue.py` (a hook runs the competing action between decision and write): stale complete vs reap+reclaim, pause/block vs claim, reaper vs heartbeat, fail vs reaper. **Mutation-checked:** dropping the WHERE conditions fails all 4 (plus 5 existing). Concurrent-edit note: the other session changed `enqueue_task` while this was in progress; the replaced part was byte-for-byte the reviewed version, so nothing of theirs was lost. Full suite 289 passed. Postgres not run (X.1).) Task state changes race (stale worker can overwrite a newer attempt): `heartbeat`/`complete_task`/`fail_task`/`block_claimed_task` check ownership in Python (`_owned_running_task`: status running + attempt match) and then write by primary key, with no row lock and no condition in the UPDATE. Reproduced on scratch SQLite by interleaving in that gap: A claims attempt 1 → lease expires, reaper requeues → B claims attempt 2 → A's `complete_task` returns True and the task ends `completed` while B is running it (B's later complete is then refused). Same shape: `pause_task`/`block_task` vs a concurrent claim can mark a running task paused/blocked; worker `fail_task` vs the reaper can double-count. Fix: lock in `_owned_running_task` (`db.get(Task, id, with_for_update=True)`) and lock the row in pause/block/resume, or use conditional UPDATEs (`WHERE status='running' AND attempts=:attempt`) and check rowcount; add interleaving tests like M0.5.15's. Found in review of M0.6.2 (rest of the review clean: claim order/`run_after`/lease, backoff math and cap, max-attempts → `failed` with audit, error truncation, blocked never claimed and no release API; 13/13 queue tests, full suite 273 passed) — `P1` — done; detail: `docs/plan-archive/tasks-done.md`

  - [x] **M2.4.3** Settle a reviewer conflict by owner/supervisor adjudication — `P1` — deps: M2.4.1, M2.4.2b — owner: @claude — since: 2026-10-12 — owner decisions 2026-10-12 (Decisions log, two rows) — needs review (P1, by someone other than @claude): **Backend.** `POST /api/projects/{p}/screening/decisions/adjudicate` (owner or supervisor; same body as `/decisions`) appends a `ScreeningDecision` with `decided_by="arbiter"` (no migration: no constraint on the column, 7 chars). `screening.adjudication(rows)` = the latest arbiter row, standing only while no reviewer has acted since it (a later reviewer decision or undo reopens the conflict). `dual_screen_status` returns a new `"adjudicated"` (with the ruling as the representative row) for a disagreeing pair that has a standing ruling; `settled_decision`, `passed_title_abstract`, the queue counts and `thematic_clusters` treat it like `"agreed"`, so PRISMA, exit conditions and exports count the ruling and its reason code. Arbiter rows are ignored by `human_decisions_by_decider`/`final_decision`, so `stage_agreement`'s kappa still compares the two reviewers' own decisions; its `conflicts` items now carry `adjudication`. **Rules chosen in the build (not in the owner's answer — for review):** only under dual-screen; only a source currently in conflict (or already ruled, to rule again); someone with a decision of their own on that source cannot rule on it; exclusions need an `E` code like any decision; refused when G3 is approved; a ruling is a screening-decision artifact like any other; audited `screening.adjudicated` (ids and decision only). `GET .../agreement` no longer blinds conflicts from callers who cannot screen (owner decision above). Audit/export README mentions `decided_by="arbiter"`. **UI:** the Screening screen's "Disagreements between reviewers" panel shows the ruling and, for an owner or supervisor who did not decide the record, "Rule: Include / Maybe / Exclude…" (reason picker for exclude), "Rule again" once ruled. Tests: 9 new in `test_screening.py` (ruling settles and counts with its reason in PRISMA; both reviewer rows and the kappa pair untouched; only owner/supervisor; a participant cannot rule; only a real conflict and only under dual-screen; exclusion code required; a reviewer changing their answer reopens the conflict; later ruling replaces earlier; locked refuses), the M2.3.3 blinding test rewritten for the decision above, 1 role-matrix row. Mutation-checked once: dropping the "reviewer acted since" rule fails the reopen test (restored). Browser-checked on a scratch SQLite DB (since deleted) with owner, co-author and supervisor: the owner (a participant) sees "you can't rule on it"; the supervisor sees the conflict, rules Exclude with reason E1, PRISMA then shows 1 excluded (E1), 0 conflicts, no console errors. **Not checked:** the "Rule again" and Maybe/Include buttons in the browser; PostgreSQL; a real third-party reviewer flow; `screen.js`'s new conflict code is not covered by the JS tests (X.58).   - Review 2026-10-11 (independent reviewer session): **FAIL, not accepted, stays `[?]`.** Fail-open window in `app/prescreen.py::run_prescreen`: the AI `ScreeningDecision` is committed (~line 200) before the sampling check and the `AutoExcludeAudit` insert (~lines 206–222). A failure in between (worker killed, DB error on the count) leaves an eligible exclude with no audit row. `todo` skips that source on retry (it already has an AI suggestion), so it counts as `ai_auto_excluded` forever with no sample. Reproduced with a throwaway test (patched `due_for_sample` to raise after the first commit, then a retry): 0 audit rows, queue `ai_auto_excluded: 1`; test deleted after the run. Fix: write the audit row in the same transaction as the decision (compute `due_for_sample` before the first commit), plus a regression test that fails before the fix. Other claims checked and matching code: pure eligibility/sampling/status logic (`app/ai_quality.py`, `test_ai_quality.py`); overturn returns the source to the queue; review is single-shot (DB CHECK + `before_update` guard) and WRITE-role only.

    - Review 2026-10-12 (independent reviewer, AI session, not a named human; X.51 still pending): **FAIL, not accepted, stays `[?]`.** (1) **Dual-screen blinding leak (blocking).** `screening.py::active_learning_training_texts` trains on `final_decision` (any decider's latest human row), and under dual-screen `GET .../queue` keeps a source in reviewer B's queue after reviewer A has decided it (`routers/screening.py:314`). So the classifier is trained on A's decision about the very records it then ranks for B: A's includes go to the top of B's list and A's excludes to the bottom. That reveals A's answer, which the blinding in M2.4.1 (`visible_rows`, AI suggestion withheld) exists to hide. Reproduced with a throwaway test (since deleted): dual-screen + active learning on, four sources with no shared words, co-author includes the newest and excludes the next newest, owner has decided nothing → owner's `sort=relevance` queue: `sort="relevance"`, A's include at position 0 of 7 and A's exclude at position 6 of 7 (oldest-first would put them at 5 and 4). The 2026-10-12 re-review's "ranking only, non-blocking" note understated this. Fix to re-review: under dual-screen, train only on settled (`agreed`) decisions or on the viewer's own decisions, never on another reviewer's rows for records still in the viewer's queue; add a regression test that fails today. (2) **Claim text:** the migration is `20261010_0043_active_learning.py` (revises `0042`), not `20261010_0042`. Checked and fine: the Naive Bayes math, AI rows never train, single-reviewer mode trains only on decided records (none of which are in the queue), and fallback and `sort` field tell the truth. Focused `test_active_learning`, `test_screening` and `test_role_matrix`: 674 passed, 0 failed. (a) 1,000 sources through `run_prescreen` with a stubbed model and real schema validation: app-side overhead 18.4 ms per abstract, below the 3.6 s budget; model latency is not measured and remains an explicitly recorded limitation. Reopened 2026-10-13 (`[~]`): the acceptance criterion requires real AI-assisted throughput, which the stubbed benchmark does not prove. Closure needs a run against a real model. (b) 500 generated 5-page PDFs through `extract_pdf_text` + section-scoped `find_quote`: 11.7 s against a 3,600 s budget, all 500 found. Full `pytest -q` → 3156 passed, 6 skipped, 0 failed. **Blocked 2026-10-20:** the 1,000/hour budget is owner-bound to the third-party API path; closing needs one live `run_prescreen` run with `LLM_API_KEY` and `LLM_API_BASE_URL` set, which the sandbox does not have (checked as booleans only). Local Ollama result (113.8/hour) is in the 2026-10-19 report.
  - Real-model run 2026-10-19 (@claude): `milestone-reports/X.19-real-model-benchmark-2026-10-19.md`. The only live model reachable in this sandbox is a local Ollama server (`qwen2.5:7b-instruct`, CPU inference) — no third-party `LLM_API_KEY`/`LLM_API_BASE_URL` is configured here, so only the local-model path (`OpenAICompatibleLLM.local`, M0.8.4's participant-data route) was measured; the default/third-party path remains unmeasured. Ran the real `app/prescreen.py::run_prescreen` through a real `OpenAICompatibleLLM` against the live endpoint for 12 synthetic title/abstract pairs: 12/12 suggestions, 379.6 s total, **31.6 s/abstract → 113.8 abstracts/hour sequential**, ~11% of the 1,000/hour budget — confirmed not an app-overhead artifact (two isolated single-call checks measured 28–38 s against the same endpoint). Stub-vs-budget regression tests unchanged and still green. **Still not closed:** the only real model available here misses the budget by ~9×; whether that is acceptable depends on (1) which model path the spec's 1,000/hour figure is meant to bind — plausibly the faster third-party path, unmeasurable here — and (2) whether the local/participant-data path is expected to hit 1,000/hour at all, which is a product-owner call, not an engineering one. Node web suite `node --test backend/app/web/js/test/*.mjs`: 84 passed, 0 failed. Defect: `write.js:161` evaluates `window.localStorage` **outside** the try/catch in `readOriginColors`; when browser storage is blocked (e.g. cookies disabled) the property access itself throws, so the Write step fails to render instead of falling back to on. The unit test passes a stub object, so it cannot catch this. Suggested fix (not applied, author's code): read the store inside the try, or wrap the accessor; add a test with a throwing `window.localStorage` getter that expects `true`.

| 2026-10-01 | Port, don't import, ARC's literature clients; write native `httpx` connectors in research-main behind the spec interface | claude (gap analysis) | ARC clients are search-only, use process-global rate/cache state, no common interface; spec requires official APIs only |
| 2026-10-01 | ARC kept only for web search / crawl / PDF extraction via its HTTP service; its pipeline/stage code is not reused | claude (gap analysis) | ARC fails open (canned text, auto-approved gates, sanitise-not-block), host-subprocess sandbox exposes env keys; conflicts with spec principles 1–2 |
| 2026-10-01 | Phase 1 (ARC) verification (M1.1.8–1.1.10) is worked before the rest of M1 | claude | Code already written; cheaper to verify now than let it drift |

| 2026-10-05 | Citation guard (M1.10.3): an answer that cites an unverified source fails the run instead of being saved with a warning; "verified" = a person's `POST …/verify` or the automatic check (M1.10.2). The conflict is shown on the source card ("Last automatic check: Mismatch") and the audit log names who verified it | user | The researcher is the author and decision-maker |

X.31.1/X.31.2 rescoped to the per-user inbox + phone/offline capture on top of this | user | Requested a minimal step-by-step UI with idea pop-ups now |
| 2026-10-06 | X.31.1 (quick capture) built ahead of X.31.6/Q14–Q22 at the user's explicit instruction ("start any task"); X.31.2 onward still wait for the walkthrough and the owner's answers | user | None of X.31.1's design choices (kind=typed/clip only, per-user not project-scoped, no AI involved) depend on an unanswered question |
| 2026-10-07 | M0.5.9 "replaced" rule: a stale artifact at stage S counts as replaced once a *current* artifact of the same `kind` produced at S is registered after it went stale (`created_at > stale_at`). A project may not advance into or past S while any stale artifact produced at S is unreplaced (409 listing them). | user (picked the proposed rule) | Chosen 2026-10-07 in chat; can be tightened later (e.g. same `ref_id` lineage) |
| 2026-10-07 | M0.5.10 gate ordering: a gate can be decided (approved or rejected) only when every earlier gate (G1…G(n-1)) is approved; approving ahead in sequence is allowed (e.g. G1 then G2 while at `idea`). Revocation: a person with the gate's required role may **reopen** an approved gate with a mandatory reason only while the project has not yet reached that gate's stage, and only if no later gate is approved (reopen later ones first — no cascade); the gate goes back to pending, queued/blocked tasks it released are blocked again, audited with the previous decision. At or past the stage, owner re-entry stays the only way back. Existing out-of-order approvals are left as they are. | user (picked both recommended options; no-cascade rule added by claude) | Chosen in chat 2026-10-07 |
| 2026-10-08 | M0.8.8: `LOCAL_LLM_API_BASE_URL` must point at a local/private host — loopback, private (10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16, IPv6 ULA), link-local, `localhost`, `*.local`/`*.internal`/`*.localhost`, or a single-label name — judged from the URL text (no DNS lookup); anything else is a configuration error unless `LOCAL_LLM_ALLOW_PUBLIC_HOST=true` is set explicitly (e.g. a lab GPU server on a public hostname) | user | Participant data must not reach a third-party model through a mislabelled 'local' setting; the override keeps self-hosted servers on public addresses usable, but only as a deliberate choice. |
| 2026-10-10 | Q2 answered: M1.4.7 should implement Scopus, Web of Science, and IEEE Xplore | user | The user selected all three licensed database APIs for the shared connector interface |

| 2026-10-10 | X.40 decision 1: M3 (extraction) backend work may start while X.14.2 (wireframe walkthrough) and X.15 (gold set) are still pending, ON CONDITION that every "red" item in the X.40 report's §2.1 table closes first (the CI-proof reviews, the AI-exclude audit review, the ARC security fix). No screening screen (M2.3) is built before the walkthrough. Extraction/screening quality is never called accepted without the real gold set. | user | X.40 decision 1 |
| 2026-10-10 | X.40 decision 4 (Q22 answered): approve data-driven stages (research-type TOML + `stage_plan_json` snapshot, §4 of `docs/step-by-step-journey-audit.md`), with two amendments: (a) starting now, no new code may hard-code the 19-stage order — any code that needs "the next stage" or "every stage" must go through one stage-order helper function, so the later X.43 rewrite has one call site to change, not many; (b) every shipped research type's screen must show its own stage list and which gates apply, not assume the fixed 19-stage list. | user | X.40 decision 4; Q22 |
| 2026-10-10 | X.40 decision 5: the five mandatory gates in every research type are G2 (search plan), G3 (screening result), G4 (extraction verification), G7 (design & ethics) and G11 (final sign-off). For a research type with no human participants (e.g. a pure literature review), G7 stays mandatory but its content becomes a light "no human participants" confirmation instead of a full ethics pack. A gate may never be detached from a stage that stays in a type's chain; a type may only drop an optional gate (G1, G5, G6, G8, G9, G10) by dropping its stage entirely. | user | X.40 decision 5 |
| 2026-10-10 | X.40 decision 6 (M3.14 approved): a cited sentence must carry a real quote from its source, verified word-for-word against the stored full text with the existing `FullText.find_quote()` (no new matching engine). No matching quote → shown "support not checked", a person must confirm. An AI judgement of "does this evidence support this claim" is explicitly deferred until the screening-accuracy work (M2.9/X.5) gives a way to measure the AI's error rate. | user | X.40 decision 6 |
| 2026-10-10 | X.40 decision 8 (X.38 answered): an AI research answer gets the same generic "AI suggestion card" as every other AI output in the app — Accept / Edit then accept / Discard / Ask again — not a one-off special-case approval button. (X.4.7, X.4.10) | claude (X.4 review) | open — **decision pending 2026-10-11**: owner will consult her university data-protection officer, date to follow. **Status 2026-10-18 (owner, chat): DPO not yet replied; owner has chased.** **Needed from owner:** the DPO's answer when it arrives. **Answered 2026-10-19 (owner, chat): no DPO review; owner signs off alone.** The earlier DPO consult note is superseded. Recommendation: keep the pseudonymise-and-keep-audit-trail build (X.4.7) provisional until the DPO answers; no real user or participant data before then. Recorded as **High**, a hard blocker for any real user and any participant data. Two options and their effects (not a legal recommendation): (a) pseudonymise the person, keep the audit trail under a research-integrity basis for a fixed period; (b) delete the person's data, which breaks the append-only audit trail's attribution for their rows **Answered 2026-10-19 (owner, chat): keep until the owner deletes; pseudonymise on erasure and keep the audit trail (option a).** |

| Q25 | Exit-condition levels: confirm each row's Required / Recommended / Information in `docs/stage-exit-conditions.md` (notably EC-4.2 conflicts = Required, EC-4.3 unscreened = Recommended, EC-5.4 every included study extracted = Recommended) (X.48) | claude (X.45) | **answered** 2026-10-11: approved, with the rule "Required only if it protects evidence, ethics or authorship; convenience/quality is Recommended or Information." Table re-checked against this rule: EC-4.7 (PRISMA numbers computed) downgraded Required→Recommended (a reporting-completeness condition, not an evidence/ethics/authorship protection). EC-0.4 (stage belongs to the project's chain) kept Required as a non-negotiable structural invariant — it has no "proceed with a reason" override at all, so it doesn't fit the Recommended/Information categories either; flagged here for the owner's awareness, not changed without her say |

(X.46.3) | claude (X.45) | **answered** 2026-10-11: the never-locked list (capture, drafting, exploring, reading later steps, going back) is project-wide for any role that may write — not a per-item toggle. Only the AI lock ("never send this to AI") is per item (X.46.3, unchanged) |
| Q27 | AI setting "Full" vs "Suggestions only": recommended meaning — Full = AI runs automatically on new items, Suggestions only = AI runs only when a person asks; both only ever suggest (X.31.7, ties to Q14) | claude (X.45) | **answered** 2026-10-11: Suggestions only = AI may comment, challenge, find evidence and check structure, but never supplies manuscript words. Full additionally allows reworded sentences the researcher may insert, always labelled in the writing record. Off = no text is sent to any AI |
| Q28 | Should G3/G4 approval refuse while that stage's Required conditions are unmet (recommended), or only stage advance? (X.48.3) | claude (X.45) | **answered** 2026-10-11: yes — approving G3 and G4 must itself refuse while any Required condition is unmet, not only stage advance, showing exactly which condition is missing with a one-click fix. Recommended conditions remain overridable with a short reason that is audited |
